<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01UhFpVyaQszHkvJ3mGBYrrv`</summary>

````text
1	---
2	name: reference-audit
3	description: 引用验证与审计——检查 bib 条目完整性、引用真实性、格式一致性。写论文、审查论文、提交前检查引用时使用。关键词：citation verification、引用检查、bib 审计、fake citation、引用格式。
4	scope: global
5	tags:
6	- workflow
7	- submission
8	- writing
9	- quality
10	priority: 6
11	version: 2
12	roles:
13	- agent
14	- paper_agent
15	- review_reviewer
16	- worker
17	---
18	
19	# 引用验证与审计（Reference Audit）
20	
21	## 核心原则
22	
23	**写时验证，而非写后检查。** 每添加一条引用，立即验证真实性。AI 生成的引用约 40% 存在错误——绝不信任记忆中的引用信息。
24	
25	## 触发条件
26	
27	- 写论文时添加引用
28	- Review Reviewer 审查论文引用质量
29	- 提交前最终检查
30	- 发现可疑引用（年份不对、作者不存在、找不到原文）
31	
32	## 验证流程
33	
34	### 1. 单条引用验证（写论文时）
35	
36	每次需要引用时：
37	
38	1. **WebSearch 查找**：搜索论文标题 + 第一作者
39	2. **Google Scholar 确认**：`site:scholar.google.com [论文标题] [作者]`
40	3. **核对关键信息**：标题、第一作者、年份（±1 年）、venue
41	4. **获取 BibTeX**：从 Google Scholar 或 DBLP 获取标准格式
42	5. **验证 claim**（如果引用了具体结论）：确认该 claim 确实出现在原文中
43	
44	### 2. 全文引用审计（批量检查）
45	
46	**Step 1：调用 `verify_references(paper_dir)`**
47	
48	这是首选方式。该工具自动完成：
49	- 解析所有 .bib 文件，提取结构化条目
50	- 解析所有 .tex 文件，提取 `\cite{}`/`\citep{}`/`\citet{}` 引用
51	- 交叉检查：找出 orphan（.bib 有但未引用）和 missing（引用了但 .bib 没有）
52	- 调 OpenAlex API 在线验证每条引用是否存在（返回 verified/not_found/mismatch）
53	
54	如果只需快速离线检查，传 `skip_online=True`。
55	
56	**Step 2：人工复核工具报告中的问题条目**
57	
58	重点关注：
59	- `status: not_found` — 可能是幻觉引用，用 WebSearch 二次验证
60	- `status: mismatch` — 年份等信息不一致，检查 preprint vs published 版本
61	- `missing_bib` — 正文引用了但 .bib 中不存在，编译会报错
62	- `orphan_bib` — .bib 中有但正文未引用，清理或补引用
63	
64	**Step 3：语义验证**（优先级排序）
65	1. 首先验证 Introduction 和 Related Work 中的高频引用
66	2. 然后验证 Method 中支撑技术 claim 的引用
67	3. 最后验证其余引用
68	
69	对于 OpenAlex 未找到的条目，用 WebSearch 补充验证（arXiv、DOI、DBLP）。
70	
71	### 3. 验证失败处理
72	
73	**找不到论文时**：
74	1. 检查拼写——标题或作者名是否有误？
75	2. 尝试不同查询组合（WebSearch + Google Scholar + DBLP）
76	3. 标记为 `[CITATION NEEDED]`
77	4. 明确告知——说明哪条引用无法验证
78	
79	**信息不匹配时**：
80	1. 确认是否找对了论文
81	2. 检查版本——preprint vs published 版本
82	3. 用最准确的版本更新信息
83	
84	**绝不做的事**：
85	- 从记忆生成 BibTeX——AI 生成引用 40% 错误率
86	- 跳过验证步骤
87	- 假设论文存在
88	- 对无法验证的引用不做标记
89	
90	## 常见引用问题模式
91	
92	| 问题类型 | 特征 | 风险 |
93	|---------|------|------|
94	| **幻觉引用** | 论文不存在，作者或标题是 AI 编造的 | 严重——直接影响学术诚信 |
95	| **信息错位** | 作者、年份、venue 不匹配 | 中等——reviewer 一查就发现 |
96	| **孤立引用** | .bib 有但正文没引用 | 轻微——但显得不够整洁 |
97	| **缺失引用** | 正文引用但 .bib 没有 | 编译错误——必须修复 |
98	| **Claim 不匹配** | 引用了论文但声称的结论不在原文中 | 严重——误导性引用 |
99	
100	## 输出格式
101	
102	审计完成后，生成结构化报告：
103	
104	```
105	## 引用审计报告
106	
107	**总数**: X 条引用
108	**已验证**: Y 条（OpenAlex confirmed）
109	**问题**: Z 条
110	
111	### 问题列表
112	
113	1. [severity: critical] `\cite{xxx}` — OpenAlex 未找到，WebSearch 也无结果，疑似幻觉引用
114	   - 建议：删除或替换为已验证的替代引用
115	
116	2. [severity: warning] `\cite{yyy}` — 年份不匹配（bib: 2023, OpenAlex: 2022）
117	   - 建议：更新为正确年份
118	
119	3. [severity: info] `\cite{zzz}` — bib 中存在但正文未引用
120	   - 建议：删除或在相关段落补充引用
121	```
122	
123	## 设计参考
124	
125	本 skill 的验证原则参考了 Dr. Claw (K-Dense Inc.) 的 inno-reference-audit skill。
126	
````

Based on the search results, I found information about the publication:

The paper "Can AI-Generated Text be Reliably Detected? Stress Testing AI Text Detectors Under Various Attacks" by Vinu Sankar Sadasivan, Aounon Kumar, Sriram Balasubramanian, Wenxiao Wang, and Soheil Feizi was published in Transactions on Machine Learning Research (TMLR) in 2025.

The paper can be accessed through OpenReview at https://openreview.net/forum?id=OOgsAZdFOt. The paper introduces recursive paraphrasing attacks to stress test the robustness of AI text detectors and provides a theoretical framework connecting detector performance to the distribution differences between human and AI-generated text.

Manning, Chelsea Finn · DetectGPT: Zero-Shot Machine-Generated Text Detection using Probability Curvature","url":"https://slideslive.com/39004346/detectgpt-zeroshot-machinegenerated-text-detection-using-probability-curvature"}]

I've found comprehensive information about the DetectGPT paper. Here's what the search results show:

## DetectGPT: Zero-Shot Machine-Generated Text Detection using Probability Curvature

**Authors & Publication:** Eric Mitchell, Yoonho Lee, Alexander Khazatsky, Christopher D. Manning, and Chelsea Finn presented this work at ICML 2023.

**Core Concept:** The key finding is that text sampled from an LLM tends to occupy negative curvature regions of the model's log probability function, and the authors define a new curvature-based criterion for judging if a passage is generated from a given LLM.

**Method:** This approach does not require training a separate classifier, collecting a dataset of real or generated passages, or explicitly watermarking generated text. It uses only log probabilities computed by the model of interest and random perturbations of the passage from another generic pre-trained language model (e.g., T5).

**Performance:** DetectGPT notably improved detection of fake news articles generated by 20B parameter GPT-NeoX from 0.81 AUROC for the strongest zero-shot baseline to 0.95 AUROC for DetectGPT.

**Availability:** The code and additional resources are available through the official project repository on GitHub and other academic platforms.

Based on the search results, I found the paper you were looking for. Here's the information:

## "Self-Consuming Generative Models Go MAD" - ICLR 2024

The paper is authored by Sina Alemohammad (first author), Josue Casco-Rodriguez, Lorenzo Luzi, Ahmed Imtiaz Humayun, Hossein Babaei, Daniel LeJeune, Ali Siahkoohi, and Richard G. Baraniuk.

**Summary:**

The paper addresses how advances in generative AI have led to the temptation to use AI-synthesized data to train next-generation models, creating an autophagous ("self-consuming") loop whose properties are poorly understood.

**Main Findings:**

The primary conclusion is that without enough fresh real data in each generation of an autophagous loop, future generative models are doomed to have their quality (precision) or diversity (recall) progressively decrease. This condition is termed Model Autophagy Disorder (MAD), by analogy to mad cow disease.

**Publication Details:**

The paper was presented at the 12th International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR 2024) in Vienna, Austria. The research demonstrates that appreciable MADness can occur in only a handful of generations.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

I found the paper you're looking for! Here's what I found:

## "Diversity Boosts AI-Generated Text Detection"

The paper by Advik Raj Basani and Pin-Yu Chen was accepted to TMLR '26 and ICML, Data in Generative Models (DIG-BUG) '25.

### Key Information:

**Authors:** Advik Raj Basani from Birla Institute of Technology and Science, Goa and Pin-Yu Chen from IBM Research, USA

**Main Contribution:** The paper proposes DivEye, a framework that captures how unpredictability fluctuates across text using surprisal-based features, motivated by the observation that human-authored text exhibits richer variability in lexical and structural unpredictability than LLM outputs.

**Performance:** DivEye outperforms existing zero-shot detectors by up to 33.2%, matches fine-tuned baselines, and boosts existing detectors by up to 18.7% when used as an auxiliary signal.

**Publication Details:** The paper was initially submitted on September 23, 2025, with the latest version dated February 25, 2026.

The paper is available on arXiv (2509.18880) and has an official implementation on GitHub at IBM/diveye, along with a demonstration website.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

I found the paper you're looking for. Here are the key details:

Koppel, M., Schler, J. and Argamon, S. (2009), Computational Methods in Authorship Attribution, JASIST, 60 (1): 9-26

The paper discusses how statistical authorship attribution has a long history, culminating in the use of modern machine learning classification methods, though most prior work suffers from assuming a small closed set of candidate authors and essentially unlimited training text for each.

The paper addresses three scenarios where traditional solutions are inadequate: the profiling problem (no candidate set), the needle-in-a-haystack problem (many thousands of candidates), and the verification problem (one suspect with no closed candidate set).

The paper has been highly influential in the field, with 783 citations according to Semantic Scholar. It is available through Wiley Online Library and other academic databases, with DOI: 10.1002/asi.20961.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

I found the paper you're looking for! Here are the key details:

**Paper Title:** Position: On the Possibilities of AI-Generated Text Detection

**Authors:** Souradip Chakraborty, Amrit Bedi, Sicheng Zhu, Bang An, Dinesh Manocha, and Furong Huang

**Publication:** Proceedings of the 41st International Conference on Machine Learning, PMLR 235:6093-6115, 2024

**Main Findings:** 

While detecting machine-generated text becomes harder as it nears human quality, it remains possible with adequate text data. The study provides evidence that differentiating human-written text from LLM outputs is consistently feasible, except when human and machine text distributions are indistinguishable across their entire support.

The paper introduces guidelines on the required text data quantity, either through sample size or sequence length, for reliable AI text detection, through derivations of sample complexity bounds.

**Empirical Evaluation:** Comprehensive empirical tests were conducted across various datasets (Xsum, Squad, IMDb, and Kaggle FakeNews) and with several state-of-the-art text generators (GPT-2, GPT-3.5-Turbo, Llama, Llama-2-13B-C

The paper is available at the official ICML proceedings page: https://proceedings.mlr.press/v235/chakraborty24a.html

I found comprehensive information about Fast-DetectGPT. Here's a summary:

## Fast-DetectGPT: ICLR 2024 Paper

Fast-DetectGPT is an ICLR 2024 paper titled "Efficient Zero-Shot Detection of Machine-Generated Text via Conditional Probability Curvature", authored by Guangsheng Bao, Yanbin Zhao, Zhiyang Teng, Linyi Yang, and Yue Zhang.

### Key Innovation

The paper introduces the concept of conditional probability curvature to elucidate discrepancies in word choices between LLMs and humans within a given context, and presents Fast-DetectGPT, an optimized zero-shot detector, which substitutes DetectGPT's perturbation step with a more efficient sampling step.

### Performance Results

Fast-DetectGPT surpasses DetectGPT by a relative around 75% in both the white-box and black-box settings and accelerates the detection process by a factor of 340. More specifically, it flags 80% of ChatGPT-crafted content, while only misidentifying 1% of human compositions.

### Resources

The code is available on GitHub at the repository mentioned in the search results, and the paper has been published in the ICLR 2024 proceedings.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Based on my search results, I found the paper you're looking for. Here's what I found:

## Overview

"Does Writing with Language Models Reduce Content Diversity?" is a paper by Padmakumar and He, presented at the 12th International Conference on Learning Representations (ICLR 2024) in Vienna, Austria.

## Key Findings

The research measured the impact of co-writing on diversity through a controlled experiment where users wrote argumentative essays in three setups—using a base LLM (GPT3), a feedback-tuned LLM (InstructGPT), and writing without model help. The study found that writing with InstructGPT (but not with GPT3) resulted in a statistically significant reduction in diversity.

Specifically, it increased the similarity between the writings of different authors and reduced the overall lexical and content diversity.

## Important Nuance

The effect is mainly attributable to InstructGPT contributing less diverse text to co-written essays, suggesting that the recent improvement in generation quality from adapting models to human feedback might come at the cost of more homogeneous and less diverse content.

The paper is available on arXiv and through the ICLR 2024 proceedings.

Based on the search results, I found comprehensive information about DNA-GPT. Here's what the research presents:

## Overview

DNA-GPT (Divergent N-Gram Analysis) is a novel training-free detection strategy for identifying GPT-generated text. The paper is authored by Xianjun Yang, Wei Cheng, Yue Wu, Linda Ruth Petzold, William Yang Wang, and Haifeng Chen and was presented at ICLR 2024.

## Methodology

The method works by truncating a text in the middle and using only the preceding portion as input to LLMs to regenerate the new remaining parts. By analyzing the differences between the original and new remaining parts through N-gram analysis in black-box or probability divergence in white-box, the researchers can illustrate significant discrepancies between machine-generated and human-written text.

## Performance

The team conducted extensive experiments on advanced LLMs from OpenAI (including text-davinci-003, GPT-3.5-turbo, and GPT-4) as well as open-source models such as GPT-NeoX-20B and LLaMa-13B. Their zero-shot approach exhibits state-of-the-art performance in distinguishing between human and GPT-generated text on four English and one German dataset, outperforming OpenAI's own classifier, which is trained on millions of text.

## Additional Features

The methods provide reasonable explanations and evidence to support detection claims, which is a unique feature of explainable detection. The method is also robust under revised text attacks and can additionally solve model sourcing.

Based on the web search results, here's what I found about "Strong Model Collapse" by Dohmatob et al. at ICLR 2025:

## Overview

"Strong Model Collapse" is a paper that, within the scaling laws paradigm which underpins the training of large neural networks like ChatGPT and Llama, establishes a strong form of the model collapse phenomenon, a critical performance degradation due to synthetic data in the training corpus.

## Key Findings

The results show that even the smallest fraction of synthetic data (e.g., as little as 1 per 1000) can still lead to model collapse: larger and larger training sets do not enhance performance.

## Model Size Investigation

The paper investigates whether increasing model size, an approach aligned with current trends in training large language models, exacerbates or mitigates model collapse. In a simplified regime where neural networks are approximated via random projections of tunable size, the authors both theoretically and empirically show that larger models can amplify model collapse.

## Authors and Status

The paper was authored by Elvis Dohmatob, Yunzhen Feng, Arjun Subramonian, and Julia Kempe, and it was published as a spotlight paper at ICLR 2025.

The paper provides both theoretical analysis and empirical validation across toy settings, realistic language model settings, and other experimental configurations to support its findings about how synthetic data in training corpora can lead to significant performance degradation.
